   - B) Madras University
   - C) Calcutta University
   - D) Allahabad University
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Sadler Commission was setup to investigate the severe structural issues, overcrowding, and performance limitations of Calcutta University, but its recommendations were applied widely across India.
Why others are wrong: Bombay, Madras, and Allahabad universities faced similar issues, but they were not the specific subjects of the Sadler inquiry.

17. **Which Viceroy was in office when the tragic Jallianwala Bagh massacre occurred and the Rowlatt Acts were passed into law?**
   - A) Lord Hardinge II
   - B) Lord Chelmsford
   - C) Lord Reading
   - D) Lord Irwin
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Lord Chelmsford served as Viceroy from 1916 to 1921, a period that witnessed the passage of the repressive Rowlatt Act and the resulting Jallianwala Bagh massacre in Amritsar in April 1919.
Why others are wrong: Lord Hardinge II left office in 1916; Lord Reading served from 1921 to 1926; Lord Irwin took office in 1926.

18. **The Lee Commission, appointed by the British Government in 1923, dealt primarily with which crucial aspect of imperial administration?**
   - A) The restructuring of the Indian Civil Services and ethnic quotas
   - B) The fortification of borders against Soviet expansion
   - C) The financial division of resources between Center and Provinces
   - D) The development of standard gauge railway networks
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Royal Commission on the Superior Civil Services in India, led by Lord Lee, recommended that future recruitment to the Indian Civil Service should be 50% British and 50% Indian over a 15-year period.
Why others are wrong: Border defense was managed by military commanders; financial distribution was studied later by the Niemeyer Committee; railways were studied by the Acworth Committee.

19. **The Simon Commission, a statutory body consisting exclusively of British Members of Parliament, was appointed during the viceroyalty of whom?**
   - A) Lord Reading
   - B) Lord Irwin
   - C) Lord Willingdon
   - D) Lord Linlithgow
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Simon Commission was appointed in late 1927 and arrived in India in 1928 during Lord Irwin's tenure to review the working of the Government of India Act 1919 and suggest constitutional changes.
Why others are wrong: Lord Reading's term ended in 1926; Lord Willingdon became Viceroy in 1931; Lord Linlithgow oversaw the implementation of provincial autonomy starting in 1936.

20. **The Butler Committee (1827), formally known as the Indian States Committee, was constituted to examine and clarify what relationship?**
   - A) The boundaries between British India and neighboring Burma
   - B) The operational relationship between the Governor-General and the Secretary of State
   - C) The relationship between the British Paramountcy and the Indian Princely States
   - D) The commercial trade treaties between the East India Company and China
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Butler Committee was appointed to investigate the nature of the relationship between the British Crown and the native states, recommending ways to improve economic and political ties while keeping paramountcy intact.
Why others are wrong: Burma boundaries were fixed by specific treaties; internal executive relationships were defined by regular constitutional acts; China trade issues were resolved by Charter Acts.

21. **Which British Viceroy issued the 'August Offer' in 1940, trying to secure Indian cooperation in World War II by promising dominion status after the war?**
   - A) Lord Willingdon
   - B) Lord Linlithgow
   - C) Lord Wavell
   - D) Lord Mountbatten
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Lord Linlithgow issued the August Offer in August 1940, which proposed expanding the Governor-Generalâ€™s Executive Council and establishing a constituent assembly after the war, but it was rejected by the Congress.
Why others are wrong: Lord Willingdon was Viceroy in the early 1930s; Lord Wavell handled the mid-war period and the Cabinet Mission; Lord Mountbatten was the final Viceroy who executed partition.

22. **The famous 'Deepavali Declaration' of October 1929, which stated that the natural issue of India's constitutional progress was the attainment of Dominion Status, was made by whom?**
   - A) Lord Reading
   - B) Lord Irwin
   - C) Lord Birkenhead
   - D) Ramsay MacDonald
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Lord Irwin issued the Deepavali Declaration on October 31, 1929, to reduce political tensions by formally stating the British government's ultimate commitment to granting Dominion Status to India.
Why others are wrong: Lord Reading had left office; Lord Birkenhead was the Secretary of State known for challenging Indians to write a constitution; Ramsay MacDonald was the Prime Minister who later issued the Communal Award.

23. **The Sargent Plan of 1944, a major policy memorandum prepared near the end of the British rule, presented a comprehensive outline for the development of what?**
   - A) Post-war industrial manufacturing hubs
   - B) National highway infrastructure and bridges
   - C) National education systems over a 40-year target period
   - D) Modernization of the Royal Indian Navy
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Sargent Report (Post-War Educational Development in India) outlined a framework to achieve universal literacy in India within 40 years, covering primary, high school, and university education.
Why others are wrong: Industrial expansion was planned under the separate Bombay Plan; highways were under the Nagpur Plan; naval upgrades were done via ad-hoc wartime defense budgets.

24. **Which committee or commission recommended the separation of railway finances from the general budget of the Central Government in 1921?**
   - A) The Acworth Committee
   - B) The Hilton Young Commission
   - C) The Muddiman Committee
   - D) The Whitley Commission
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Acworth Committee, appointed in 1920 under Sir William Acworth, recommended separating railway finances from the general budget, a practice that began in 1924 and continued for over 90 years.
Why others are wrong: Hilton Young Commission recommended setting up the Reserve Bank of India; Muddiman Committee examined the failures of provincial dyarchy; Whitley Commission investigated labor working conditions.

25. **Who was the Governor-General of India during the execution of the First Anglo-Sikh War (1845-1846) which resulted in the Treaty of Lahore?**
   - A) Lord Ellenborough
   - B) Lord Hardinge I
   - C) Lord Dalhousie
   - D) Lord Gough
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Lord Hardinge I served as Governor-General during the First Anglo-Sikh War and successfully imposed the humiliating Treaty of Lahore on the Sikh empire in 1846.
Why others are wrong: Lord Ellenborough had left office in 1844; Lord Dalhousie arrived later and fought the Second Anglo-Sikh War; Lord Gough was a military commander, not the Governor-General.

26. **The Raleigh Commission of 1902, which paved the way for the controversial Indian Universities Act of 1904, was appointed by which Viceroy?**
   - A) Lord Elgin II
   - B) Lord Curzon
   - C) Lord Minto II
   - D) Lord Hardinge II
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Lord Curzon appointed the Universities Commission under Sir Thomas Raleigh to inspect the condition of higher education institutions, which led to tight official government control over universities.
Why others are wrong: Lord Elgin II preceded Curzon; Lord Minto II and Hardinge II governed long after the Indian Universities Act was already in effect.

27. **Which committee was appointed in 1924 to investigate the operational defects and political deadlocks caused by the system of 'Dyarchy' in provincial governments?**
   - A) The Muddiman Committee
   - B) The Simon Commission
   - C) The Linlithgow Committee
   - D) The Sapru Committee
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Reforms Inquiry Committee, headed by Sir Alexander Muddiman, was set up in 1924 to evaluate the practical administrative difficulties arising from the introduction of dyarchy under the 1919 Act.
Why others are wrong: The Simon Commission was a larger statutory group set up in late 1927; Linlithgow headed an agricultural committee; Sapru Committee was an independent Indian body looking at constitutional outlines later.

28. **The system of 'Dual Government' (Diarchy) in Bengal, where the British held the military power and revenue rights while the Nawab held judicial responsibility, was introduced by whom?**
   - A) Warren Hastings
   - B) Robert Clive
   - C) Lord Cornwallis
   - D) John Shore
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Robert Clive introduced the Dual Government in Bengal after securing the Diwani rights in the Treaty of Allahabad (1765), leaving the puppet Nawab with administrative accountability but no real power.
Why others are wrong: Warren Hastings abolished this highly corrupt Dual Government system in 1772; Cornwallis and Shore focused on the permanent land settlement reforms.

29. **Which committee was set up by the Government of India in 1929 to investigate the growth and deficiencies of education under the changing constitutional setup?**
   - A) The Hartog Committee
   - B) The Wardha Scheme Committee
   - C) The Wood-Abbott Committee
   - D) The Radhakrishnan Commission
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Hartog Committee was appointed as an auxiliary committee of the Simon Commission to review the expansion of education, highlighting the high rate of wastage and stagnation at the primary level.
Why others are wrong: Wardha Scheme was Mahatma Gandhiâ€™s basic education plan (1937); Wood-Abbott evaluated vocational training in 1937; Radhakrishnan Commission was formed post-independence in 1848.

30. **Who was the Viceroy of India when the British capital was officially shifted from Calcutta to Delhi during a grand Imperial Durbar in 1911?**
   - A) Lord Curzon
   - B) Lord Minto II
   - C) Lord Hardinge II
   - D) Lord Chelmsford
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Lord Hardinge II hosted the Delhi Durbar in December 1911 in honor of King George V, where the historic decisions to annul the partition of Bengal and shift the capital to Delhi were announced.
Why others are wrong: Lord Curzon resigned in 1905; Lord Minto II left office in 1910; Lord Chelmsford assumed office later in 1916.

## Modern - Social Reform (Raja Ram Mohan Roy, Vivekananda, Dayanand, Jyotiba Phule, Ambedkar) (Modern)

1.  Topic Introduction
    The given questions focus on Modern Indian History, specifically covering the Socio-Religious Reform Movements of the 19th and 20th centuries. This encompasses the intellectual awakening led by reformers who challenged social evils like Sati, the caste hierarchy, untouchability, and gender discrimination while introducing rationalism, education, and modern constitutional ideals. This topic forms a mandatory core segment of the General Awareness (History) syllabus for the SSC CGL exam.

2.  Core Concepts Explained Simply  - Brahmo Samaj: Founded to propagate monotheism and eliminate orthodox Hindu rituals, focusing heavily on human dignity and opposition to social evils like Sati.
  - Arya Samaj: A reform movement advocating a return to pure Vedic traditions, rejecting idol worship, and introducing social changes through modern education.
  - Ramakrishna Mission: An organization combining ancient spiritual philosophy with practical humanitarian relief and social service.
  - Satyashodhak Samaj: A radical anti-caste movement aimed at liberating lower castes and women from Brahmanical hegemony and institutional exploitation.
  - Dalit Emancipation Movement: A 20th-century rights movement emphasizing structural annihilation of caste, socio-political representation, and legal constitutional protections.3.  Important Facts and Dates  - 1815: Atmiya Sabha founded by Raja Ram Mohan Roy (became Brahmo Samaj in 1828).
  - 1829: Passage of the Bengal Sati Regulation Act under Lord William Bentinck.
  - 1848: First indigenous girls' school established in Pune by Jyotiba and Savitribai Phule.
  - 1873: Satyashodhak Samaj founded and the book 'Gulamgiri' published by Jyotiba Phule.
  - 1875: Arya Samaj established by Swami Dayanand Saraswati in Bombay; 'Satyarth Prakash' published.
  - 1893: Swami Vivekananda's historic speech at the Parliament of the World's Religions in Chicago.
  - 1897: Ramakrishna Mission founded by Swami Vivekananda.
  - 1924: Bahishkrit Hitakarini Sabha launched by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.
  - 1927: Mahad Satyagraha and symbolic burning of Manusmriti led by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.
  - 1932: Signing of the Poona Pact between Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and Mahatma Gandhi.
  - 1956: Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and his followers formally converted to Buddhism in Nagpur.4.  Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams  - Journals and Newspapers: SSC frequently tests publications (e.g., Sambad Kaumudi and Mirat-ul-Akhbar by Raja Ram Mohan Roy; Kesari by Tilak; Mooknayak and Bahishkrit Bharat by B.R. Ambedkar).
  - Titles Bestowed: The title 'Raja' was given to Ram Mohan Roy by Mughal Emperor Akbar II; 'Mahatma' was given to Jyotirao Phule by Vithalrao Krishnaji Vandekar on behalf of the public of Bombay.
  - Splitting of Societies: Ideological splits within the Brahmo Samaj (Adi Brahmo Samaj under Debendranath Tagore vs. Brahmo Samaj of India under Keshub Chandra Sen).
  - Political Ventures: Political parties founded by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar including the Independent Labour Party (1936) and the Scheduled Castes Federation (1942).5.  Differences Between Similar Terms  - Separate Electorates vs. Reserved Seats: Separate electorates allowed only community members to vote for their specific candidates, whereas reserved seats meant a seat was held for a specific community candidate but voted on by the general electorate (as settled by the Poona Pact).
  - Arya Samaj vs. Brahmo Samaj: Arya Samaj looked backward to the authority of the Vedas to revive Hinduism, whereas Brahmo Samaj integrated Western rationalism with Upanishadic monotheism.
  - Daridra Narayana vs. Shuddhi: Daridra Narayana is a service concept recognizing God within the poor (Vivekananda), while Shuddhi was a formal purification ritual intended to reconvert individuals back to Hinduism (Arya Samaj).6.  Examples  - Anti-Sati Legislation: The direct outcome of Raja Ram Mohan Roy's persistent advocacy and journalistic writing in 'Sambad Kaumudi'.
  - Dalit Civil Rights Assertion: The Mahad Satyagraha served as a classic example of non-violent collective agitation specifically targeting the social restrictions placed on public drinking water.
  - Educational Syncretism: Dayanand Anglo-Vedic (D.A.V.) schools illustrate a synthesis of traditional Vedic spiritual values alongside modern English-medium scientific education.7.  Previous-Year Exam Perspective  - SSC CGL exams constantly test exact dates of establishment, founders of prominent socio-religious organizations, and specific literary works. Questions heavily target the functional titles of reformers, legal acts associated with social changes, and events detailing the Dalit rights movement.8.  Quick Revision Points and Mnemonics  - Sati Prohibition 1829: Banned by Bentinck backed by Raja Ram Mohan Roy (B.R. = Bentinck + Roy).
  - Arya Samaj: Slogan "Back to the Vedas" and text 'Satyarth Prakash' belong to Dayanand Saraswati.
  - Phule's Works: Satyashodhak Samaj (1873) and 'Gulamgiri' (1873) highlight the fight against slavery/exploitation.
  - Ambedkar's Milestone: Mahad Satyagraha and Manusmriti Dahan took place in the same year (1927).
  - Persian Weekly: Mirat-ul-Akhbar = Mirror of News by Raja Ram Mohan Roy.

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **Who founded the Atmiya Sabha in 1815, which later developed into the Brahmo Samaj for social and religious reform?**
   - A) Swami Vivekananda
   - B) Dayanand Saraswati
   - C) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - D) Jyotiba Phule
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Raja Ram Mohan Roy founded the Atmiya Sabha in 1815 to propagate monotheism and reform Hindu society, laying the groundwork for the Brahmo Samaj in 1828.
Why others are wrong: Swami Vivekananda founded the Ramakrishna Mission; Dayanand Saraswati established the Arya Samaj; Jyotiba Phule founded the Satyashodhak Samaj.

2. **Which prominent social reformer was instrumental in the passage of the Bengal Sati Regulation Act of 1829?**
   - A) B.R. Ambedkar
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Swami Vivekananda
   - D) Dayanand Saraswati
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Raja Ram Mohan Roy campaigned tirelessly against the practice of Sati, leading Lord William Bentinck to officially ban it in 1829.
Why others are wrong: B.R. Ambedkar focused on Dalit rights and the Constitution in the 20th century; Swami Vivekananda and Dayanand Saraswati focused on Hindu revitalization and education later in the 19th century.

3. **The slogan "Back to the Vedas" was raised by which Indian social and religious reformer?**
   - A) Jyotiba Phule
   - B) Dayanand Saraswati
   - C) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - D) Swami Vivekananda
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Swami Dayanand Saraswati believed the Vedas were the source of infallible truth and called for a return to Vedic ideals to purify Hinduism.
Why others are wrong: Jyotiba Phule critiqued Vedic scriptures as sources of Brahmanical hegemony; Raja Ram Mohan Roy synthesized Western rationalism with Upanishadic monotheism; Swami Vivekananda emphasized Practical Vedanta.

4. **Which socio-religious organization was established by Swami Dayanand Saraswati in Bombay in 1875?**
   - A) Brahmo Samaj
   - B) Ramakrishna Mission
   - C) Satyashodhak Samaj
   - D) Arya Samaj
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: Dayanand Saraswati founded the Arya Samaj in 1875 to promote Vedic values, reject idol worship, and encourage social reforms like women's education.
Why others are wrong: Brahmo Samaj was established by Raja Ram Mohan Roy; Ramakrishna Mission by Swami Vivekananda; Satyashodhak Samaj by Jyotiba Phule.

5. **Representing Hinduism, Swami Vivekananda delivered his famous speech at the Parliament of the World's Religions in which city?**
   - A) London
   - B) New York
   - C) Chicago
   - D) Paris
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Swami Vivekananda gained international recognition after his historic address at the Parliament of Religions in Chicago in September 1893.
Why others are wrong: London, New York, and Paris were cities he visited later during his global tours, but they did not host the 1893 Parliament of Religions.

6. **Which organization did Swami Vivekananda found in 1897 to carry out humanitarian relief and social service?**
   - A) Arya Samaj
   - B) Ramakrishna Mission
   - C) Theosophical Society
   - D) Prarthana Samaj
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Swami Vivekananda established the Ramakrishna Mission in 1897, named after his guru Ramakrishna Paramahansa, to combine spiritual ideals with social service.
Why others are wrong: Arya Samaj was founded by Dayanand Saraswati; Theosophical Society by Madame Blavatsky and Col. Olcott; Prarthana Samaj by Atmaram Pandurang.

7. **Jyotiba Phule founded the Satyashodhak Samaj in 1873 primarily to achieve which social goal?**
   - A) Universal adoption of Vedic rituals
   - B) Liberating the lower castes from Brahmanical exploitation
   - C) Promoting classical Sanskrit education among elite women
   - D) Supporting the political demands of the Indian National Congress
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Satyashodhak Samaj (Truth-Seekers' Society) was formed by Phule to challenge the caste hierarchy and champion the rights of Shudras and Ati-Shudras.
Why others are wrong: Phule rejected Vedic rituals as exploitative; he prioritized vernacular primary education for the masses over elite Sanskrit education; the Indian National Congress was formed later in 1885.

8. **The celebrated book 'Gulamgiri' (Slavery), published in 1873, was written by which social reformer?**
   - A) B.R. Ambedkar
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Jyotiba Phule
   - D) Dayanand Saraswati
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Mahatma Jyotiba Phule wrote 'Gulamgiri' to draw parallels between the plight of lower castes in India and the struggles of African American slaves in the US.
Why others are wrong: B.R. Ambedkar wrote books like 'Annihilation of Caste'; Raja Ram Mohan Roy wrote 'Tuhfat-ul-Muwahhidin'; Dayanand Saraswati authored 'Satyarth Prakash'.

9. **Who was the chief architect of the Indian Constitution and served as independent India's first Law Minister?**
   - A) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - B) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
   - C) Jyotiba Phule
   - D) Swami Vivekananda
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Dr. B.R. Ambedkar chaired the Drafting Committee of the Constituent Assembly and shaped the constitutional framework of modern India.
Why others are wrong: Raja Ram Mohan Roy lived during the British East India Company era; Jyotiba Phule passed away in 1890; Swami Vivekananda passed away in 1902, long before independence.

10. **Dr. B.R. Ambedkar launched the Bahishkrit Hitakarini Sabha in 1924 with which core objective?**
   - A) Translating the Vedas into Marathi
   - B) Promoting education and socio-economic upliftment among the depressed classes
   - C) Coordinating labor strikes in British textile factories
   - D) Reviving traditional temple dance forms
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Bahishkrit Hitakarini Sabha was established by Ambedkar to remove difficulties of the untouchables and place their grievances before the government.
Why others are wrong: Ambedkar did not advocate for Vedic study; factory labor strikes were handled by specific unions later; temple dance revival was championed by artists like Rukmini Devi Arundale.

11. **Which social reformer established the first school for girls in Pune at Budhwar Peth in 1848 alongside his wife Savitribai Phule?**
   - A) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - B) Jyotiba Phule
   - C) Dayanand Saraswati
   - D) B.R. Ambedkar
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Jyotiba Phule and Savitribai Phule opened India's first indigenous school for girls in Pune in 1848, braving immense social orthodox backlash.
Why others are wrong: Raja Ram Mohan Roy operated primarily in Bengal; Dayanand Saraswati's educational networks (DAV) expanded later in the late 19th century; Ambedkar's educational initiatives occurred in the 20th century.

12. **The Mahad Satyagraha of 1927, led by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar, fought for which fundamental right?**
   - A) Entry into the Kalaram Temple
   - B) The right of untouchables to draw water from the public Chaudar Tank
   - C) Reduction of land revenue taxes for farmers
   - D) Representation in the Viceroy's Executive Council
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Mahad Satyagraha was a pioneering civil rights movement led by Ambedkar to assert the right of Dalits to use water from a public tank in Mahad.
Why others are wrong: Kalaram Temple entry movement happened in 1930; land revenue reduction was a focus of peasant movements like Bardoli; political council representation was negotiated at the Round Table Conferences.

13. **The modern educational institutions known as D.A.V. (Dayanand Anglo-Vedic) schools were established to honor the legacy of which reformer?**
   - A) Swami Vivekananda
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Dayanand Saraswati
   - D) Jyotiba Phule
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The D.A.V. College Trust and Management Society was established in 1886 to realize Dayanand Saraswati's vision of blending Vedic values with modern scientific education.
Why others are wrong: Vivekananda's educational legacy is managed through Ramakrishna Mission schools; Raja Ram Mohan Roy helped establish the Hindu College; Jyotiba Phule's legacy lies in primary mass schools in Maharashtra.

14. **Which reform leader publically burned the 'Manusmriti' in 1927 as a symbolic protest against caste inequality?**
   - A) Jyotiba Phule
   - B) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
   - C) Dayanand Saraswati
   - D) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: On December 25, 1927, Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and his followers burned the Manusmriti during the Mahad Satyagraha to reject the religious sanction of untouchability.
Why others are wrong: Jyotiba Phule heavily critiqued ancient texts but did not publically burn Manusmriti in 1927; Dayanand Saraswati sought to reform Hinduism within a Vedic paradigm; Raja Ram Mohan Roy worked through legislative and editorial critiques.

15. **Raja Ram Mohan Roy founded a weekly newspaper in Persian titled what?**
   - A) Sambad Kaumudi
   - B) Mirat-ul-Akhbar
   - C) Kesari
   - D) Harijan
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: 'Mirat-ul-Akhbar' (Mirror of News) was a Persian weekly journal established by Raja Ram Mohan Roy in 1822 to discuss political and social issues.
Why others are wrong: Sambad Kaumudi was his Bengali weekly; Kesari was founded by Bal Gangadhar Tilak; Harijan was started by Mahatma Gandhi.

16. **Swami Vivekananda's guru, whose teachings emphasized that all religions lead to the same ultimate reality, was who?**
   - A) Ramakrishna Paramahansa
   - B) Swami Dayanand
   - C) Devendranath Tagore
   - D) Guru Nanak
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Ramakrishna Paramahansa was a mystic priest of Dakshineswar whose teachings on the unity of all religions deeply transformed Vivekananda.
Why others are wrong: Swami Dayanand was the founder of Arya Samaj; Devendranath Tagore was a leader of the Brahmo Samaj; Guru Nanak was the founder of Sikhism.

17. **In which year did Dr. B.R. Ambedkar formally renounce Hinduism and convert to Buddhism at Nagpur?**
   - A) 1947
   - B) 1950
   - C) 1956
   - D) 1962
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Dr. B.R. Ambedkar converted to Buddhism along with hundreds of thousands of his followers on October 14, 1956, at Deekshabhoomi in Nagpur.
Why others are wrong: 1947 was the year of independence; 1950 marked the adoption of the Constitution; Ambedkar passed away in December 1956, making 1962 inaccurate.

18. **The social purification ritual called 'Shuddhi', aimed at reconverting individuals back to Hinduism, was initiated by which organization?**
   - A) Brahmo Samaj
   - B) Arya Samaj
   - C) Satyashodhak Samaj
   - D) Ramakrishna Mission
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Arya Samaj introduced the Shuddhi movement to bring back individuals who had converted to other religions like Islam or Christianity into Hinduism.
Why others are wrong: Brahmo Samaj rejected traditional conversion rituals; Satyashodhak Samaj was anti-Brahmanical and focused on caste rights; Ramakrishna Mission focused on service rather than active proselytization.

19. **Who gave Jyotirao Phule the title of 'Mahatma' in 1888 in recognition of his immense service to the marginalized?**
   - A) The British Governor of Bombay
   - B) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
   - C) The public of Bombay, through social activist Vithalrao Krishnaji Vandekar
   - D) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Social activist Vithalrao Krishnaji Vandekar, on behalf of the public of Bombay, bestowed the title 'Mahatma' upon Jyotiba Phule in May 1888.
Why others are wrong: The British government gave certificates of honor but not the title Mahatma; Tilak had political differences with Phule's radical anti-caste stances; Ambedkar was a young child in 1888 and regarded Phule as one of his gurus later.

20. **The influential text 'Satyarth Prakash' (The Light of Truth), detailing the principles of monotheism and Vedic authority, was authored by whom?**
   - A) Swami Vivekananda
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Swami Dayanand Saraswati
   - D) B.R. Ambedkar
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: 'Satyarth Prakash' was written by Swami Dayanand Saraswati in 1875 to elucidate the doctrines of the Arya Samaj and critique various religious practices.
Why others are wrong: Vivekananda wrote books like 'Raja Yoga' and 'Jnana Yoga'; Raja Ram Mohan Roy wrote tracts against Sati and idols; Ambedkar wrote extensive socioeconomic and political treaties.

21. **Which organization underwent a split into the 'Adi Brahmo Samaj' and the 'Brahmo Samaj of India' due to internal ideological differences?**
   - A) Arya Samaj
   - B) Brahmo Samaj
   - C) Satyashodhak Samaj
   - D) Ramakrishna Mission
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Brahmo Samaj split in 1866 when Keshab Chandra Sen parted ways with Devendranath Tagore, forming the Brahmo Samaj of India, while Tagore's faction became the Adi Brahmo Samaj.
Why others are wrong: Arya Samaj later split into the College and Gurukul factions; Satyashodhak Samaj and Ramakrishna Mission did not undergo this specific internal schism.

22. **Dr. B.R. Ambedkar submitted a memorandum demanding separate electorates for the depressed classes at which historic event?**
   - A) The Surat Split of 1907
   - B) The Round Table Conferences in London (1930-1932)
   - C) The Shimla Conference of 1945
   - D) The Mountbatten Plan negotiations
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Ambedkar strongly advocated for the political rights and separate electorates for Dalits during the Round Table Conferences, leading to the Communal Award.
Why others are wrong: The Surat Split concerned Congress Moderates and Extremists; the Shimla Conference focused on the Wavell Plan; the Mountbatten Plan dealt directly with India's partition.

23. **The 'Poona Pact' of 1932 was signed between Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and which other leader, altering electoral seats for the depressed classes?**
   - A) Jawaharlal Nehru
   - B) Mahatma Gandhi
   - C) Subhas Chandra Bose
   - D) Lord Irwin
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Following Gandhi's fast unto death against the Communal Award, the Poona Pact was signed between Ambedkar and Hindu leaders, abandoning separate electorates in favor of increased reserved seats for Dalits.
Why others are wrong: Nehru and Bose were not the primary signatories or focus of this negotiation; Lord Irwin was the former Viceroy, while Lord Willingdon was Viceroy during the pact.

24. **Which reformer argued that caste was not a division of labor, but rather a "division of laborers"?**
   - A) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - B) Swami Vivekananda
   - C) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
   - D) Dayanand Saraswati
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: In his undelivered speech 'Annihilation of Caste', Dr. B.R. Ambedkar critiqued the defense of the caste system, stating it artificially splits workers into rigid hierarchies.
Why others are wrong: Raja Ram Mohan Roy focused on religious monotheism; Vivekananda viewed caste as a social institution that needed spiritual egalitarianism; Dayanand favored a reformed Chaturvarna based on merit, not birth.

25. **The Ved Samaj, established in Madras in 1864, was directly inspired by the ideals of which northern reform movement?**
   - A) Arya Samaj
   - B) Brahmo Samaj
   - C) Ramakrishna Mission
   - D) Satyashodhak Samaj
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Ved Samaj in Southern India was deeply influenced by the Brahmo Samaj, working to reform rituals, promote widow remarriage, and eliminate caste distinctions.
Why others are wrong: Arya Samaj was founded later in 1875; Ramakrishna Mission in 1897; Satyashodhak Samaj operated primarily in Maharashtra with different structural roots.

26. **Savitribai Phule, who championed women's liberation alongside Jyotiba Phule, is widely recognized as India's first what?**
   - A) Female High Court Judge
   - B) Female Prime Minister
   - C) Female Modern School Teacher
   - D) Female Member of Parliament
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Savitribai Phule broke patriarchal taboos to become India's first female teacher at the girls' school established in Pune in 1848.
Why others are wrong: Anna Chandy was the first female judge; Indira Gandhi was the first female Prime Minister; Parliament membership came post-independence.

27. **Which reformer traveled to England in 1830 as an ambassador of the Mughal Emperor Akbar II and passed away in Bristol in 1833?**
   - A) Dayanand Saraswati
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Swami Vivekananda
   - D) B.R. Ambedkar
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Raja Ram Mohan Roy was given the title of 'Raja' by Mughal Emperor Akbar II and traveled to England to argue for the Emperor's pension, where he eventually died and was buried.
Why others are wrong: Dayanand Saraswati never traveled to Europe; Vivekananda traveled to the US and Europe much later; Ambedkar went to England for higher studies and conferences in the 20th century.

28. **The concept of 'Daridra Narayana'â€”serving God by serving the poor and destituteâ€”was popularized by whom?**
   - A) Jyotiba Phule
   - B) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
   - C) Swami Vivekananda
   - D) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Swami Vivekananda coined and popularized 'Daridra Narayana' to emphasize that service to humanity, especially the downtrodden, is the highest form of worship.
Why others are wrong: Phule's focus was socio-political liberation; Roy's focus was legal and intellectual religious purification; Ambedkar's focus was constitutional rights and systemic annihilation of caste.

29. **What political party did Dr. B.R. Ambedkar found in 1936 to contest elections and protect labor rights?**
   - A) Justice Party
   - B) Independent Labour Party
   - C) Scheduled Castes Federation
   - D) Communist Party of India
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Ambedkar formed the Independent Labour Party in 1936, which achieved significant success in the 1937 provincial elections in Bombay.
Why others are wrong: Justice Party was active in Madras; Scheduled Castes Federation was formed by him later in 1942; Communist Party of India was an independent Marxist organization.

30. **The 'Tattvabodhini Sabha', formed to propagate the systematic study of India's past and Upanishadic texts, was established by whom before merging into the Brahmo Samaj?**
   - A) Keshub Chandra Sen
   - B) Debendranath Tagore
   - C) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
   - D) Dayanand Saraswati
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Debendranath Tagore established the Tattvabodhini Sabha in 1839, which later merged its resources and leadership into the Brahmo Samaj to sustain the reform movement.
Why others are wrong: Keshub Chandra Sen joined the Brahmo Samaj later; Vidyasagar focused heavily on widow remarriage in Bengal; Dayanand Saraswati was the head of the Arya Samaj.
